This workspace contains three folders. care_docs, care_fe, care.

In this task, let's create an agent and skill that the agent can use to generate
documentation for the care_docs folder. The agent will be responsible for analyzing the contents of the care_docs folder and generating comprehensive documentation based on the files and their structure.

Basically I should be able to select: "Care Documentation Orchestrator" agent which will include the required skill to generate documentation. This agent should be able to spin up multiple sub-agents to run things in parallel as well if supported (example: concept, and each flows could be written in parallel by orchestrating multiple parallel sub-agents), I mean you decide what's the best fastest way to do things. Use Claude Fable 5 model for researching/understanding/analyzing things. And for sub-agents to write things to the documentation, use Claude Opus 5. I'd do that, but if you think there's a more efficient way to do things, do let me know!

When generating docs, there are couple of rules to follow.

- Care is an FHIR R5 compliant HMIS software. So when describing anything, it should be in the context of FHIR R5. Always reference/link to it's source. That's the source of truth.
- Always refer to `~/ohc.network/care` (backend) `~/ohc.network/care_fe` (frontend) for any implementation details or code references. This will ensure that the documentation is accurate and aligned with the actual codebase.
- Documentation content should always STRICTLY follow Simplified Technical English (STE) guidelines. This means using clear, concise, and unambiguous language. Avoid complex sentences and jargon. Use active voice and present tense whenever possible. ASD-STE100 standard should be followed for all documentation.
- There are two main types of documentation to generate for every module or component in the care_docs folder:
  1. **Concept**: This section should provide a high-level overview of the module or component. It should explain what the module does, key attributes, its purpose, and how it fits into the overall system. Include any relevant FHIR R5 concepts and references to the source documentation.
  2. **Flow**: This section should provide a detailed explanation of the module's workflow, including how it interacts with other components in the system. It should include step-by-step explanations of processes. Reference any relevant code in `~/ohc.network/care` and `~/ohc.network/care_fe` to illustrate implementation details. Analyse the codebase and figure out all possible flows and interactions and ask the user for any missing information or clarifications if needed. NEVER assume or guess about the implementation details. Always verify with the actual codebase and ask the user for confirmation if something is slightly unclear or ambiguous.
- Concepts and Flows are user facing documentation and not technical documentation. Hence, enums and choices such as `status`, `type`, `category`, etc. should be represented in user-readable manner, and not what is used in the codebase. For example, if a status is represented as `1` in the codebase, it should be documented as `Active` or `Inactive` based on its meaning in the context of the application. Or for example, `in_progress` should be documented as `In Progress`. Always ensure that the documentation is understandable to end-users and stakeholders who may not have technical expertise. Refer the i18n's en.json file for any user-facing strings to ensure consistency in terminology and phrasing across the documentation.
- Documentation hierarchy would be 
  - Parent (eg. Clinical, user must provide this, if not ask.)
    - Module/Component Name (eg. Patient, user must provide this, if not ask.)
      - concept.md
      - flows
        - <flow_name>.md
        - <flow_name>.md

Here is an example of a concept documentation for Clinical - Patient:
```markdown
# Clinical - Patient (this should be centered using HTML align=center)

## Definition

(This section should provide a clear and concise definition of the module or component. It should explain what the module does, its purpose, and how it fits into the overall system. Include any relevant FHIR R5 concepts and references to the source documentation.)

A **patient**(link it with FHIR R5: https://build.fhir.org/patient.html) in Care is a person who receives care through your facility or program. It is the longitudinal anchor for clinical documentation- every encounter, observation, order, and care plan links back to a patient record.

## Key Attributes 
Key attributes stored within patient includes

Components | What it captures
---|---
Name | Legal and preferred names used to identify the person across the record.
Phone Number | Mobile number with country code for communication
Emergency Contact | People to reach on the patient's behalf.
Sex | The patient's gender identity
Date of Birth/ Age | Date of birth where known, or age as on date of the registration.
Blood Group | Blood group of the patient
Patient Tag | Deployment defined labels applied to the patient for classification and identification
Address | Address of correspondence
Pincode | Postal code of the patient
State | State of the patient
District to Local Body | District to ward details of the patient
Deceased Status | Whether the patient is deceased, with date of death where recorded
Identifier | Unique ID used for patient identification within the instance

### Identifier

Patients can be identified in the instance using one or more identifiers that can be configured as per the deployments requirements.

Identifiers must be unique and can be auto generated or manually updated. 

### Status

A patient record moves through a small set of states. Registration is the entry point that creates the record; the states below describe where it sits afterwards.

Status | Description
---|---
Active | The patient record is active
Deceased | The patient record is marked as deceased

## Related
Reference: Patient (technical, link to other docs)
Flow: Create a patient (link to flow.md)
Playbook: Outpatient registration (HMIS, link to playbook)
```

Here is an example of a flow documentation for Clinical - Patient: How to create a patient:
```markdown
# How to Create a Patient (this should be centered using HTML align=center)

## Overview

This flow describes how to register a new [patient](link to concept of patient) in Care manually.

## Pre-requisites

(This section should list any pre-requisites or conditions that must be met before the flow can be executed. It should include any necessary permissions, roles, or configurations required to perform the flow. For example, to create an encounter, you first need a patient to be registered.)

(example:)
- The logged in user must be part of the facility
- Required permissions for creating a patient must be there

## Permissions

(Always refer the backend's RBAC implementation for permissions and authorize_* functions in relevant viewsets. Analayze the backend's codebase and specify how to look for permissions so that agents can find the required permissions easily without again having to read the entire codebase to analyze how RBAC works.)

Permission | Access
---|---
Can create patient | Allows the user to create a new patient.
Can list patient | Allows the user to view the list of patients.

## Steps

### 1. Open Patient Registration Form

From the facility dashboard, select Patients from the left navigation menu.
- Click Search Patients.
- Select Add New Patient to start a new registration.

### Enter Patient Details

Complete the patient registration form - The form is divided into two sections:
- **Patient Basics**: Enter the patient's primary demographic information, including:

Components | What it captures
---|---
Name | Legal and preferred names used to identify the person across the record.
Phone Number | Mobile number with country code for communication
Emergency Contact | People to reach on the patient's behalf.
Sex | The patient's gender identity
Date of Birth/ Age | Date of birth where known, or age as on date of the registration.
Blood Group | Blood group of the patient
Patient Tag | Deployment defined labels applied to the patient for classification and identification
- **Additional Details**: Provide any additional information required by your deployment, such as:

Components | What it captures
---|---
Address | Address of correspondence
Pincode | Postal code of the patient
State | State of the patient
District to Local Body | District to ward details of the patient
Deceased Status | Whether the patient is deceased, with date of death where recorded
Identifier | Unique ID used for patient identification within the instance

Note: Which fields are mandatory depends on your deployment’s configuration. (Always refer to care_config to know if there are configurations that affects behaviour and include it it notes or relevant steps)

### Register the Patient
- Click the Register button to submit the form.
- You could also use the keyboard shortcut `Ctrl + Enter` to submit the form. (always refer keyboard shortcuts in care_fe and include it in the steps if applicable)

### Expected Outcome
- A patient is created with the details entered.
- A patient ID is assigned according to the deployment's and facility identifier    configuration.

### Related

Concepts: 
 - Patient
 - ...

Flows: 
 - Search for a patient
```

- Analyze the codebase in this session itself and add instructions on how to find the relevant code references in the backend and frontend for each flow. This will help users understand where to look for implementation details and how to navigate the codebase effectively. So that we can skip analyzing the codebase again for every flow. For example, keyboard shortcuts, add instructions on how to find the relevant code references in the frontend for keyboard shortcuts, and for permissions, add instructions on how to find the relevant code references in the backend for permissions. These are instructions for the agent, and not the output documentation.

### Identifier

Patients can be identified in the instance using one or more identifiers that can be configured as per the deployments requirements.

Identifiers must be unique and can be auto generated or manually updated. 

### Status

A patient record moves through a small set of states. Registration is the entry point that creates the record; the states below describe where it sits afterwards.

Status | Description
---|---
Active | The patient record is active
Deceased | The patient record is marked as deceased

## Related
Reference: Patient (technical, link to other docs)
Flow: Create a patient (link to flow.md)
Playbook: Outpatient registration (HMIS, link to playbook)
```

Here is an example of a flow documentation for Clinical - Patient: How to create a patient:
```markdown
# How to Create a Patient (this should be centered using HTML align=center)

## Overview

This flow describes how to register a new [patient](link to concept of patient) in Care manually.

## Pre-requisites

(This section should list any pre-requisites or conditions that must be met before the flow can be executed. It should include any necessary permissions, roles, or configurations required to perform the flow. For example, to create an encounter, you first need a patient to be registered.)

(example:)
- The logged in user must be part of the facility
- Required permissions for creating a patient must be there

## Permissions

(Always refer the backend's RBAC implementation for permissions and authorize_* functions in relevant viewsets. Analayze the backend's codebase and specify how to look for permissions so that agents can find the required permissions easily without again having to read the entire codebase to analyze how RBAC works.)

Permission | Access
---|---
Can create patient | Allows the user to create a new patient.
Can list patient | Allows the user to view the list of patients.

## Steps

### 1. Open Patient Registration Form

From the facility dashboard, select Patients from the left navigation menu.
- Click Search Patients.
- Select Add New Patient to start a new registration.

### Enter Patient Details

Complete the patient registration form - The form is divided into two sections:
- **Patient Basics**: Enter the patient's primary demographic information, including:

Components | What it captures
---|---
Name | Legal and preferred names used to identify the person across the record.
Phone Number | Mobile number with country code for communication
Emergency Contact | People to reach on the patient's behalf.
Sex | The patient's gender identity
Date of Birth/ Age | Date of birth where known, or age as on date of the registration.
Blood Group | Blood group of the patient
Patient Tag | Deployment defined labels applied to the patient for classification and identification
- **Additional Details**: Provide any additional information required by your deployment, such as:

Components | What it captures
---|---
Address | Address of correspondence
Pincode | Postal code of the patient
State | State of the patient
District to Local Body | District to ward details of the patient
Deceased Status | Whether the patient is deceased, with date of death where recorded
Identifier | Unique ID used for patient identification within the instance

Note: Which fields are mandatory depends on your deployment’s configuration. (Always refer to care_config to know if there are configurations that affects behaviour and include it it notes or relevant steps)

### Register the Patient
- Click the Register button to submit the form.
- You could also use the keyboard shortcut `Ctrl + Enter` to submit the form. (always refer keyboard shortcuts in care_fe and include it in the steps if applicable)

### Expected Outcome
- A patient is created with the details entered.
- A patient ID is assigned according to the deployment's and facility identifier    configuration.

### Related

Concepts: 
 - Patient
 - ...

Flows: 
 - Search for a patient
```

I've reviewed the plan and analyzed if the usage of the models for each purpose is efficient.

Short answer: yes. Fable 5 is more powerful than most of this pipeline needs. You use it for the orchestrator and the 2 researchers. Neither role needs the top tier. Move them down. Keep the strong model only where it earns its cost.

### Recommended model map

Role | Plan model | Better model | Reason
Orchestrator | Fable 5 | Sonnet 5 | It coordinates and merges. It does not need the top tier.
Researcher | Fable 5 | Sonnet 5 | It reads code and builds a dossier. Sonnet 5 is enough.
Writer | "Opus 5" | Opus 5 | It writes the docs. The text quality matters.
Reviewer | "Opus 5" | Opus 5 | It checks all docs in 1 pass. This step needs the strong model.

### Why Fable 5 is the wrong pick for research

Fable 5 has safety classifiers. The classifiers route cybersecurity, biology, chemistry, and distillation prompts to an Opus 4.8 fallback, and you pay Fable 5 rates for that Opus 4.8 output. Your researchers read RBAC, permission, and auth code. This code can trigger the cybersecurity classifier. So Fable 5 can give you Opus 4.8 work at a higher price. [Tygart Media](https://tygartmedia.com/claude-models-comparison/)

Fable 5 is also slow. Complex tasks take 60 seconds to several minutes, against 3 to 15 seconds for Opus 4.8. Your researchers run in parallel and run often. This delay adds up. [Tygart Media](https://tygartmedia.com/claude-models-comparison/)
